Cap position weight at 4% of portfolio until Q3 2026 earnings resolve the central debate. Do not add on daily TD-9 completion — it is a tactical signal, not a strategic one; wait for weekly TD-9 approach or the Q3 print before increasing exposure. Time horizon: through Q3 2026 earnings (~October 2026), which is the single catalyst that can decisively resolve the CapEx/FCF/AI-attribution debate.

Investment Thesis: The three-analyst debate converges on Hold after five rounds, and the convergence is driven by specific evidence rather than analytical fatigue.

Why Hold is correct, not just acceptable: The neutral analyst's probability-weighted valuation is the decisive framework. Assigning roughly 35-40% probability to forward EPS of $34.70 (the bull case), 30% to $30, 20% to $27, and 10-15% to $24 or below produces a probability-weighted forward EPS of ~$30-31. At $549.90, that is a forward P/E of ~17.7-18.3 for a company growing revenue at 20-28% with 81.4% gross margins. This is a fair valuation — not the deep value the aggressive analyst sees at 15.85x (which requires the full bull case), and not the impaired valuation the conservative analyst implies (which requires extrapolating the worst quarter of the CapEx cycle as a permanent baseline). Fair valuation with balanced optionality in both directions is the textbook definition of a Hold.

The bull case that almost won — and why it fell short: The aggressive analyst's strongest argument is that META's 28% revenue growth on a $200B base, expanding gross margins, and a 0.82 PEG represent genuine undervaluation. The depreciation math correction was also meaningful — incremental depreciation from CIP coming online is $11-16B annually, not $66B. However, the conservative analyst won two critical sub-arguments that neutralize the bull case. First, the $6-12B cushion between incremental gross profit ($39B at 20% growth) and the total expense stack ($27-33B from depreciation, R&D at decelerated 15%, SBC at decelerated 20%, and interest expense) is thin — a 2% miss on revenue growth or R&D failing to decelerate as projected erases it entirely. Second, R&D and SBC have their own momentum driven by headcount and AI talent competition with OpenAI, Anthropic, and Google — they do not mechanically decelerate when CapEx plateaus. The aggressive analyst's assumption of synchronous deceleration across all expense lines is the weakest link in the forward EPS defense.

The bear case that was overstated — but identified genuine risks: The conservative analyst's "leverage spiral in formation" language was correctly dismantled by both colleagues. Operating cash flow of $31.86B/quarter (up 24.6% YoY) is real cash generation, not an accounting artifact. The debt issuance is a choice to accelerate the build-out, not a necessity to cover operating losses. However, the conservative analyst's two strongest points stand: (1) the business model transition from capital-light to capital-intensive means ROIC has structurally declined — deploying $120B in CapEx for $15-20B in incremental gross profit produces 12-17% ROIC versus historical capital-light returns — and the market has not fully repriced this transition; and (2) the GPU refresh cycle means the post-build-out CapEx baseline is permanently elevated, not a reversion to pre-cycle levels.

The unresolved binary risk that prevents directional conviction: The legal proceedings targeting potential divestiture of Instagram and WhatsApp represent a fat left tail that cannot be quantified with current data. The aggressive analyst's point that the stock rose during the week of the $200B trial headline suggests the market is pricing the risk, not ignoring it. The conservative analyst's counter that "looking beyond a risk is not resolving it" is equally valid. Both are right, which is precisely why directional conviction is unwarranted and the put hedge at $500 is essential hedging hygiene.

Technical considerations support patience, not action: The weekly TD-9 at 4/9 carries more structural weight than the daily/monthly counts at 6/9. The August 14th bounce failure (MACD histogram flipped positive then rolled back to negative within four sessions) is evidence that buyers lack conviction, not that sellers are exhausting. The ADX surge from 4.56 to 21.47 describes a nascent trend that has room to mature before exhaustion. Adding capital on a lower-tier tactical signal while the higher-tier signal says the primary trend has room to continue would be confirmation bias, not risk management.

The catalyst that resolves the debate: Q3 2026 earnings (~October 2026) is the single data point that can decisively resolve the CapEx/FCF/AI-attribution debate. Specifically: (a) Does CapEx begin to plateau below $30B/quarter? (b) Does FCF recover above $5-8B/quarter? © Does management provide any AI revenue attribution? (d) Does revenue growth sustain at or above 25%? Until that print arrives, both the bull and bear frameworks are running on inference and interpretation, and the optimal strategy is to maintain exposure, floor the tail risk, and let the data — not conviction — determine the next move.
